### 3.4 P8: residency you can see, and a CLI that does not fight the dev server

**Change.**

- `client.residency()` returns rows and bytes per table on the device, with
  the store's total bytes and, on the web, the wasm heap. The guide's residency
  card shows it next to the sync windows that bound it.
- `snapback4 run <job>` and the other store-writing CLI commands detect a live
  dev server on the same store (its lifetime lock file) and either run the work
  through that server or refuse with `E_STORE_BUSY`, naming the server's
  address, instead of failing with `database is locked`. Which of the two is
  the lane's first finding.

**Evidence.** Residency on a fixture with known rows; the CLI against a running
dev server, both ways.

**2026-10-08 — `run` goes through a live dev host (app-farm round 1).** The
lane's first finding was the refusal; 27 of ~270 app-farm builds (41 diaries)
then stopped their dev server to run one operation, or reverse-engineered the
wire. `run` now takes the other branch of this item when the owner is a
responsive `snapback4 dev` (it answers `GET /dev/status`): a query or mutation
goes to `POST /q|/m/<op>` with the persona header naming the same principal a
local run would use, and a job to the dev-only `POST /dev/jobs/<job>`, which
queues it behind earlier runs and drives chunks on the dev clock. No second
writer opens the store. `--now`, a non-persona principal, `serve` and a stopped
host still refuse `E_STORE_BUSY`, now with the equivalent `curl`. Other
store-writing commands keep the refusal.

**2026-09-27 — Darwin ownership correction (lane OW).** Ownership now locks
`<database filename>-owner` beside the canonical database, a regular 0600 file
opened with `O_NOFOLLOW`: exclusive for backend writers, shared for devices
including the native C ABI. On macOS/iOS, a flock on the database itself
conflicts with SQLite's POSIX locks, preventing startup adoption and device
commits. Database identity remains `(dev, ino)`, checked with stat alone;
only a missing database is created and closed before SQLite opens it. The
guard holds the sidecar, never a database descriptor whose close could discard
SQLite's locks. Sidecars persist after unlock and move with the database's
other sidecars; symlinks, hard links and nonregular ownership files are refused.
`serve.lock` remains an address hint, and `E_STORE_BUSY` keeps its meaning.

**2026-09-27 — round-1 file-mutation hardening (lane OW).** Both ownership
and address-file opens lstat the database and candidate before opening and
compare the opened inode again before any lock, read, write or chmod. A
post-open alias or uncertain descriptor is deliberately forgotten, never
closed or unlocked: either operation could discard SQLite's POSIX locks.
Ownership sidecars must be empty, and address records at most 128 bytes;
these stat-only bounds also refuse a SQLite database renamed to either name
when its original path is absent. Lock the sidecar before creating a missing
database; an `E_STORE_BUSY` probe creates no database or address record.
Database hard links are refused with `E_STORE` and “copy the store, do not
hard-link it,” including on an existing owner's path check.

**2026-09-27 — round-2 exclusion boundary (lane OW, option b).** Exclusion
holds while `<database>-owner` is left in place. Deleting or replacing it while
a process runs ends exclusion: a second owner can create and lock a new sidecar
while the first can continue reading and writing. Removal does not revoke a
running process or cancel its transactions. The retained descriptor still locks
the old inode, which no longer coordinates new opens. Stop all owners before
moving a store and move its sidecars with it; leave `-owner` in place for the
whole ownership lifetime.

This replaces round 1's partial fail-closed claim and removes its live sidecar
checks. `Store::conn()` exposes a raw SQLite connection: auth autocommit writes,
auth and worker transactions, and effect/action/job/timeline reads bypass the
store's transaction helpers. There is no single existing fallible interception
point; changing that accessor's return type would change the connection API and
its callers, and an accessor check cannot revoke a previously borrowed
connection. No single-digit-microsecond transaction-cost claim is made. The
database inode, hard-link and acquisition-time sidecar checks remain. Unit,
native-device and two-process regressions label unlink/replacement as this
documented boundary and prove a second owner can start while old work continues.

**2026-09-27 — live token mutual proof (lane TK, round 1).** A busy
`token` command asks the announced owner to mint through its existing writer.
The per-run 0600 `<canonical database>-token` secret never crosses HTTP:
BSD wildcard listeners need not own the specific loopback address the CLI
dials. Both rounds require an actual loopback socket peer, one literal
loopback Host, no Origin and single protocol headers. IPv4-mapped IPv6 peers
fail closed, including mapped loopback; native `::1` remains usable.

The client sends a fresh 32-byte OS nonce and the request. The server issues a
fresh 32-byte nonce and HMAC-SHA256 over `snapback4-token-server`, client nonce,
server nonce and request binding. Only after verifying it does the client send
HMAC-SHA256 over `snapback4-token-client`, server nonce, client nonce and the
same binding. Nonces are fixed-width raw bytes within the MAC, hex on the wire;
the HMAC key is the exact 64 ASCII hex bytes in the private file. The binding
is UTF-8 JSON of `[1, service, days, storeIdentity]`; days bind before clamping.
The identity hashes JSON of `[hex(canonicalPathBytes), device, inode]`, avoiding
`/var` versus `/private/var` aliases and lossy path encoding. Different stores
also have independent per-run secrets.

pub(super) fn bulk(shared: Arc<Shared>) {
    loop {
        if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { return; }
        let observed = *lock(&shared.seq);
        let mut did_work = false;
        for _ in 0..3 {
            if crate::lifecycle::stopping() { return; }
            did_work |= bulk_slice(&shared, false);
            let pause = lock(&shared.maintenance).resume_at.saturating_duration_since(Instant::now());
            if !pause.is_zero() {
                // No shared lock survives this pause. Request commits do not
                // cancel repayment; empty kinds do not idle an active cycle.
                std::thread::sleep(pause.min(Duration::from_millis(100)));
            }
        }
        if !did_work {
            loop {
                let pause = lock(&shared.maintenance).empty_cycle(Instant::now());
                if pause.is_zero() { break; }
                std::thread::sleep(pause.min(Duration::from_millis(100)));
            }
            let seq = lock(&shared.seq);
            if *seq == observed {
                drop(shared.committed.wait_timeout(seq, Duration::from_millis(100)).unwrap_or_else(|e| e.into_inner()));
            }
        }
    }
}

// Reset even when a debug/unwind request panics mid-drain. Engine poison
// recovery and durable work cursors retain the normal retry semantics.
struct Advancing<'a>(&'a Shared);
impl Drop for Advancing<'_> {
    fn drop(&mut self) {
        let mut state = lock(&self.0.maintenance);
        state.advancing = false;
        state.next = BulkKind::Delivery;
    }
}

fn completed_drain(work: Work, stopping: bool) -> Result<Work, snapback4_core::Refusal> {
    if stopping {
        return Err(snapback4_core::Refusal::new("E_CLOCK_INTERRUPTED", "clock", "clock advanced, but host shutdown interrupted the drain; inspect queued work instead of repeating the advance").guide("clock"));
    }
    Ok(work)
}

pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    if !shared.personas { return respond(request, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::release())})); }
    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
        Ok(payload) => payload,
        Err(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))})),
    };
    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
        None => false,
        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
    };
    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {
        Ok(milliseconds) => milliseconds,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // Serialize clock callers only. Background slices check advancing after
    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.
    let advance = lock(&shared.maintenance).advance.clone();
    let _advance = lock(&advance);
    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
    let advanced = (|| {
        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);
        let mut clock = lock(&shared.clock);
        let mut next = clock.clone();
        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules)?;
        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;
        engine.advance_worker_leases_at(milliseconds, clock.now()).map_err(|e| e.refusal())?;
        *clock = next;
        let offset = now - snapback4_core::now_ms();
        let isolated = if crate::dev_clock_store::has_data(&engine) {
            format!("; use {} for isolated time tests; clock advance --port N targets its printed port (guide jobs)", crate::dev_clock::isolated_command())
        } else { String::new() };
        let message = format!("cumulative offset {offset:+}ms (authoritative floor included); process offset lasts until restart{isolated}");
        Ok((now, offset, message, before, boundaries))
    })();
    let (now, offset, message, before, boundaries) = match advanced {
        Ok(result) => result,
        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    };
    // @ref LLP 3000#contended-read-serving — a developer is awaiting this
    // request; synchronous clock-advance work intentionally keeps request QoS.
    let drained = (|| {
        let mut crossed = Vec::new();
        if each_boundary {
            for (index, at) in boundaries {
                crossed.extend(engine_work(shared, |engine, _| engine.fire_schedule_boundary_at(now, index, at))?);
            }
        }
        let mut work = work(shared, true, 0)?;
        work.schedules += crossed.len();
        work.fired.extend(crossed);
        Ok::<_, RuntimeError>(work)
    })();
    match drained.map_err(|error| error.refusal()).and_then(|work| completed_drain(work, crate::lifecycle::stopping())) {
        Ok(work) => {
            let schedules = crate::dev_clock_store::schedule_report(&lock(&shared.engine), &before, &work.fired, now, shared.hold_schedules, each_boundary);
            respond(request, 200, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":offset, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "work":work, "schedules":schedules}))
        }
        // Time remains advanced if maintenance fails; callers must not
        // retry the advance blindly. The normal tick retries pending work.
        Err(error) => respond(request, 500, json!({"now":now, "advancedMs":milliseconds, "offsetMs":offset, "skewMs":offset, "eachBoundary":each_boundary, "message":message, "denied":refusal_json(&error)})),
    }

fn origin_authority(origin: &str) -> Option<&str> {
    let authority = origin.strip_prefix("http://").or_else(|| origin.strip_prefix("https://"))?;
    authority_host(authority)?;
    Some(authority)
}

/// Browser developers use different local app ports. Trust literal loopback
/// names, never DNS resolution: a foreign hostname may resolve to loopback.
fn dev_request(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> Result<(), Refusal> {
    if !shared.personas { return Ok(()); }
    let host = single_header(request, "host");
    let origin = single_header(request, "origin");
    if !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|addr| addr.ip().is_loopback())
        || !request.url().starts_with('/') || request.url().starts_with("//")
        || !matches!(host, Ok(Some(host)) if local_authority(host))
        || !matches!(origin, Ok(None) | Ok(Some(_)))
        || origin.ok().flatten().is_some_and(|origin| !origin_authority(origin).is_some_and(local_authority)) {
        return Err(Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "development HTTP requires a loopback Host and a loopback browser Origin; use localhost or a literal loopback address").guide("personas"));
    }
    Ok(())
}

// @ref LLP 3000.007#20.2 — public routes bypass dev admission, never the privacy boundary.
pub(crate) fn trusted_dev_request(request: &Request, shared: &Shared) -> bool {
    shared.personas && dev_request(request, shared).is_ok()
}

pub(crate) fn cors(request: &Request) -> Vec<Header> {
    let mut headers = vec![
        Header::from_bytes("Vary", "Origin").unwrap(),
        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Expose-Headers", "X-Snapback-Time, X-Snapback-Clock, X-Snapback-Generation").unwrap(),
        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Headers", "content-type, x-snapback-persona, x-snapback-meter, authorization, range, x-snapback-device").unwrap(),
        Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Methods", "GET, POST, OPTIONS").unwrap(),
    ];
    if let Ok(Some(origin)) = single_header(request, "origin") && origin_authority(origin).is_some() {
        headers.push(Header::from_bytes("Access-Control-Allow-Origin", origin).unwrap());
    }
    if latency::headers_enabled() {
/// `token --service <name> [--days N] [--release <dir>]`: a bearer session
/// for a service principal, printed once. A service is a worker's seat: it
/// claims effects, mints sessions for the accounts the application
/// authenticates itself, and is admitted by rules as `service:<name>`.
pub fn token(options: &Options) -> i32 {
    let Some(name) = options.flags.get("service").filter(|name| !name.is_empty()) else {
        eprintln!("usage: {}", crate::cli_flags::synopsis("token"));
        return 2;
    };
    let days: i64 = options.flags.get("days").and_then(|d| d.parse().ok()).unwrap_or(365);
    let path = options.flags.get("release").map(|dir| Path::new(dir).join("store.sqlite"))
        .unwrap_or_else(|| project_or_exit().store_path());
    if !options.flags.contains_key("release") && !path.is_file() {
        eprintln!("cannot open the store: no store at {}; run `snapback4 dev` first", path.display());
        return 2;
    }
    let _owner = match crate::store_owner::command(&path) {
        Ok(owner) => Some(owner),
        Err(error) if error.starts_with("E_STORE_BUSY ") => None,
        Err(error) => { eprintln!("{error}"); return 2; }
    };
    let store_path = &path;
    let session = if let Some(owner) = &_owner {
        let store = match owner.open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)) {
            Ok(store) => store,
            Err(error) => { eprintln!("cannot open the store: {error}"); return 2; }
        };
        snapback4_core::identity::service_session(&store, name, snapback4_core::now_ms(), days)
            .map_err(|error| (1, format!("token: {error}")))
    } else {
        crate::serve::token::mint_live(&path, name, days)
    };
    match session {
        Ok(session) => {
            if options.json {
                println!("{}", json!({"token":session.token,"principal":session.principal,"expiresAt":session.expires_at,"store":store_path}));
            } else {
                println!("{}", session.token);
                if !options.flags.contains_key("quiet") {
                    eprintln!("token: a session for {} until {}; printed once; keep it where the worker keeps secrets", session.principal, session.expires_at);
                    eprintln!("store: {}; this token belongs only to this store; each isolated store needs its own", store_path.display());
                }
            }
            0
        }
        Err((status, error)) => {
            eprintln!("{error}");
            status
        }
    }
}

async function post(url, path, body = {}, token) {
  const response = await fetch(url + path, { method: 'POST', headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json', ...(token ? { authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } : {}) }, body: JSON.stringify(body) });
  return { response, body: await response.json() };
}
async function member(url, principal) {
  const seeded = await post(url, '/seed', [
    { table: 'conversations', row: { id: 'room', title: 'Clock', creatorId: principal } },
    { table: 'memberships', row: { id: 'member', conversationId: 'room', principal, role: 'admin', joinedAt: 1 } },
  ]);
  assert.equal(seeded.body.seeded, 2);
}

test('wire stamps successes and refusals on sync, changes, query, mutation and session endpoints', async t => {
  const f = await devServer({ seed: [], extraSource, clockOffset: '+25h' }); t.after(() => f.close());
  const began = Date.now();
  let revision;
  const stamped = response => {
    const at = Number(response.headers.get('x-snapback-time'));
    assert.ok(Number.isSafeInteger(at) && at >= began + 90_000_000 && at <= Date.now() + 90_000_000, `${response.url}: ${at}`);
    revision ??= response.headers.get('x-snapback-clock');
    assert.equal(response.headers.get('x-snapback-clock'), revision);
    assert.match(response.headers.get('access-control-expose-headers'), /X-Snapback-Time/);
  };
  const signed = await post(f.api, '/auth/guest'); stamped(signed.response);
  let token = signed.body.session.token;
  await member(f.api, signed.body.session.principal);
  for (const [path, body] of [
    ['/sync', { from: 0 }], ['/q/media.latestClock', { args: { conversationId: 'room' } }],
    ['/m/media.sendClock', { id: 'clock-wire', args: { conversationId: 'room', body: 'server stamp' }, now: 1 }],
    ['/q/missing', { args: {} }], ['/m/missing', { id: 'missing', args: {} }],
    ['/sync', { from: -1 }],
  ]) stamped((await post(f.api, path, body, token)).response);
  const rows = (await post(f.api, '/q/media.latestClock', { args: { conversationId: 'room' } }, token)).body.data;
  const sent = rows.find(row => row.body === 'server stamp');
  assert.ok(sent, 'read back the committed mutation row');
  assert.notEqual(sent.at, 1, 'the client-supplied mutation now is never authoritative');
  assert.ok(sent.at >= began + 90_000_000 && sent.at <= Date.now() + 90_000_000);
  stamped(await fetch(f.api + '/changes?wait=0', { headers: { authorization: `Bearer ${token}` } }));
  for (const path of ['/sync', '/q/media.latestClock', '/m/media.sendClock', '/auth/refresh', '/auth/logout-all'])
    stamped((await post(f.api, path, {}, 'invalid')).response);
  stamped(await fetch(f.api + '/changes?wait=0'));
  const rotated = await post(f.api, '/auth/refresh', {}, token); stamped(rotated.response); token = rotated.body.session.token;
  stamped((await post(f.api, '/auth/logout-all', {}, token)).response);
  stamped((await post(f.api, '/auth/logout', {}, token)).response);
  // Password disabled is still a stamped session endpoint response.
  for (const path of ['/auth/signup', '/auth/login']) stamped((await post(f.api, path, { email: 'clock@example.com', password: 'clock-password' })).response);
});

for (const tier of ['web', 'native']) test(`${tier}: a legacy admission keeps its floor through upgrade, retirement and rollback`, async t => {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'sb4-legacy-clock-')); t.after(() => rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }));
  const persist = memoryPersistence();
  const raw = tier === 'web' ? webDevice({ wasm, persist }) : processDevice({ dir, binary: binary() });
  const device = raw;
  let wall = 1_004_000, core = await device.open('legacy-clock', { initial_backend: modelBackend });
  t.after(() => core.close());
  await core.close();
  // Build the old persisted shape directly: current enqueue must write a floor.
  const entry = { id: 'legacy', seq: 1, op: 'write', args: { hiddenHandle: false, refuse: false },
    viewer: 'alice', now: wall, new_ids: [], predicted: [], predictable: false };
  if (tier === 'web') await persist.save('legacy-clock', [{ s: 'o', k: entry.id, v: JSON.stringify(entry) }]);
  else {
    const { DatabaseSync } = await import('node:sqlite');
    const db = new DatabaseSync(join(dir, 'legacy-clock.sqlite'));
    try { db.prepare('INSERT INTO _device_outbox (seq, id, entry) VALUES (?, ?, ?)').run(entry.seq, entry.id, JSON.stringify(entry)); }
    finally { db.close(); }
  }
  core = await device.open('legacy-clock', null);
  assert.equal((await core.call({ op: 'meta', key: 'server-clock-floor' })).ok, null, 'legacy admission has no floor record');
  const upgraded = new ServerClock(() => wall); await upgraded.attach(core);
  assert.equal((await core.call({ op: 'meta', key: 'server-clock-floor' })).ok, '1004000', 'attach commits the backfill before retirement');
  assert.equal((await core.call({ op: 'settle', id: 'legacy' })).denied, undefined);
  assert.deepEqual((await core.call({ op: 'queued' })).ok, []);
  await core.close(); // No clock.detach(): only committed device transactions survive.
  wall -= 4000;
  core = await device.open('legacy-clock', null);
  const reopened = new ServerClock(() => wall); await reopened.attach(core);
  assert.equal(reopened.issuedDeviceNow(), 1_004_000, 'retirement cannot erase the legacy admission floor');
});

test('web orphan recovery after attachment preserves its admission floor through settlement and rollback', async t => {
  const name = 'late-clock', orphan = `${name}:tab-old`, persist = memoryPersistence(), batches = [];
  const entry = { id: 'orphan', seq: 1, op: 'write', args: { hiddenHandle: false, refuse: false },
    viewer: 'alice', now: 1_002_000, new_ids: [], predicted: [], predictable: false };
  const later = { ...entry, id: 'orphan-later', seq: 2, now: 1_004_000 };
  await persist.save(orphan, [{ s: 'm', k: 'device:store_id', v: 'clock-store' },
    ...[entry, later].map(e => ({ s: 'o', k: e.id, v: JSON.stringify(e) }))]);
  const held = await persist.claim(orphan, false); t.after(() => held.release());
  const device = webDevice({ wasm, persist: { ...persist, async save(store, changes) {
    batches.push({ store, changes }); await persist.save(store, changes);
  } } });
  let wall = 1_000_000, core = await device.open(name, { initial_backend: modelBackend }); t.after(() => core.close());
  const observe = { op: 'observe_store', page: { snapshot: false, generation: 1, events: [], watermark: 0, more: false, store_id: 'clock-store', requested_watermark: 0 } };
  assert.equal((await core.call(observe)).denied, undefined);
  await core.call({ op: 'set_meta', key: 'server-clock-floor', value: '1002000' });
  const clock = new ServerClock(() => wall); await clock.attach(core);
  core.onOutbox(time => clock.raiseIssued(time));
  assert.equal(clock.issuedDeviceNow(), 1_002_000);
  assert.deepEqual((await core.call({ op: 'queued' })).ok, []);
  assert.equal((await core.call({ op: 'meta', key: 'server-clock-floor' })).ok, '1002000');
  await held.release();
  assert.equal((await core.call(observe)).denied, undefined); // Normal orphan recovery, after attach.
  assert.equal(await persist.has(orphan), false);
  assert.deepEqual((await core.call({ op: 'queued' })).ok.map(e => e.now), [entry.now, later.now]);
import test from 'node:test';
import assert from 'node:assert/strict';
import { mkdtempSync, writeFileSync, rmSync } from 'node:fs';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { createServer } from 'node:http';
import { loadFixtures } from '../dist/proof-fixtures.js';

test('loadFixtures resolves only typed leaves at one dev clock and returns reset-ready rows', async t => {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), 'surf3-proof-fixtures-'));
  t.after(() => rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }));
  const fields = { at: 'Time', optional: { Optional: 'Time' }, times: { List: { of: 'Time', max: 4 } }, text: { Text: { max: 40 } }, json: { Json: { max: 1000 } } };
  const requests = [];
  const server = createServer((request, response) => {
    requests.push(request.url); response.setHeader('content-type', 'application/json');
    response.end(JSON.stringify(request.url === '/dev/catalog' ? { tables: [{ name: 'events', fields }] } : { now: 100_000_000 }));
  });
  await new Promise(resolve => server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', resolve));
  t.after(() => new Promise(resolve => server.close(resolve)));
  const url = `http://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}`;
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'events.jsonl'), JSON.stringify({ id: 'e', at: 'now+2h', optional: 'now-1h', times: ['now', 'now+1ms'], text: 'now+2h', json: { at: 'now' } }) + '\n');
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'test.cases.jsonl'), 'not a row file\n');
  assert.deepEqual(await loadFixtures(dir, { url }), [{ table: 'events', row: { id: 'e', at: 107_200_000, optional: 96_400_000, times: [100_000_000, 100_000_001], text: 'now+2h', json: { at: 'now' } } }]);
  assert.deepEqual(requests.sort(), ['/dev/catalog', '/dev/status']);
  requests.length = 0;
  assert.equal((await loadFixtures(dir, { url, now: 200_000_000 }))[0].row.at, 207_200_000);
  assert.deepEqual(requests, ['/dev/catalog']);
  for (const bad of ['now+0h', 'now+wat', 'now+9007199254740991w', 'now-999999999w']) {
    writeFileSync(join(dir, 'events.jsonl'), JSON.stringify({ at: bad }));
    await assert.rejects(loadFixtures(dir, { url }), /E_FIXTURE_TIME: events.jsonl:1.at/);
  }
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'events.jsonl'), '[]\n');
  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures(dir, { url }), /E_INPUT.*expected a row object/);
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'unknown.jsonl'), '{}\n');
  writeFileSync(join(dir, 'events.jsonl'), '{}\n');
  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures(dir, { url }), /unknown table unknown/);
});

test('loadFixtures refuses bad clocks, durations and bounded catalog failure', async () => {
  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures('.', { url: 'http://127.0.0.1:1', timeoutMs: 0 }), /E_PROOF_USAGE/);
  await assert.rejects(loadFixtures('.', { url: 'http://127.0.0.1:1', timeoutMs: 1000 }), /fetch failed/);
});

    const requests = [];
    const server = createServer((req, res) => {
      requests.push([req.url, req.headers]);
      res.setHeader('content-type', 'application/json');
      res.end(JSON.stringify(req.url === '/' ? { tables: [] } : { now: 1 }));
    });
    await new Promise(resolve => server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1', resolve));
    t.after(() => new Promise(resolve => server.close(resolve)));
    await loadFixtures(dir, { url: `http://127.0.0.1:${server.address().port}`, ...credentials });
    assert.deepEqual(requests.map(([path]) => path).sort(), ['/', '/dev/status']);
    for (const [, headers] of requests) {
      if (credentials.persona) assert.equal(headers['x-snapback-persona'], 'alice');
      else assert.equal(headers.authorization, 'Bearer test');
    }
  });
}

});

   352	pub(super) fn advance(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
   353	    if !shared.personas { return respond(request, 403, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::release())})); }
   354	    let payload = match serde_json::from_str::<serde_json::Value>(body) {
   355	        Ok(payload) => payload,

   357	    };
   358	    let each_boundary = match payload.get("eachBoundary") {
   359	        None => false,
   360	        Some(serde_json::Value::Bool(value)) => *value,
   361	        Some(_) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&crate::dev_clock::input("eachBoundary must be a boolean"))})),
   362	    };
   363	    let duration = payload.get("duration").and_then(|value| value.as_str());
   364	    let milliseconds = match duration.ok_or_else(|| crate::dev_clock::input("clock advance takes {\"duration\":\"+2m\"}"))
   365	        .and_then(crate::dev_clock::duration) {

   370	    // engine admission and skip during this synchronous drain.

   372	    let _advance = lock(&advance);
   373	    let _advancing = Advancing(shared);
   374	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#20.12 — simulated time makes retries due without stealing a live worker's claim or viewer session.
   375	    let advanced = (|| {
   376	        // Match claims' engine-then-clock lock order: no claim can land
   377	        // between shifting its lease and publishing the advanced clock.
   378	        let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
   379	        let before = crate::dev_clock_store::saved_boundaries(&engine);

   381	        let mut next = clock.clone();
   382	        let now = next.advance(milliseconds)?.max(shared.time_audience_clock.load(std::sync::atomic::Ordering::Acquire));
   383	        let boundaries = crate::dev_clock_store::crossed_boundaries(&engine, &before, now, shared.hold_schedules)?;
   384	        lock(&shared.maintenance).advancing = true;

   395	        Ok(result) => result,

   423	/// `POST /dev/jobs/<job>` with `{"args":{..}}`: queue one run of a job as the
   424	/// system on the dev clock and drive chunks until it is done or parked. This is
   425	/// what `snapback4 run <job>` forwards to while dev owns the store.
   426	pub(super) fn run_job(request: Request, shared: &Shared, name: &str, body: &str) {
   427	    use snapback4_core::{JobState, error::Refusal, ir::ProgramKind, wire};
   428	    let service_route=request.url().starts_with("/jobs/");
   429	    let service=if service_route {
   430	        match super::session_identity::principal_of(&request,shared) {
   431	            Ok((principal,kind,snapback4_core::identity::SessionScope::Full)) if kind=="service" && principal.starts_with("service:") => Some(principal),
   432	            _ => return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","triggering a deployed system job requires a full service token").guide("jobs"))})),
   433	        }
   434	    } else {
   435	        if !shared.personas {return respond(request,403,json!({"denied":refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH","auth","/dev/jobs is a development door; use run <job> --release <dir> with a service token").guide("jobs"))}));}
   436	        None
   437	    };
   438	    let refuse = |request, status, refusal: Refusal| respond(request, status, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&refusal.guide("jobs"))}));
   439	    let payload: serde_json::Value = match serde_json::from_str(if body.trim().is_empty() { "{}" } else { body }) {
   440	        Ok(payload) => payload,
   441	        Err(error) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("the request body is not JSON: {error}; send {{\"args\":{{..}}}}"))),
   442	    };
   443	    if service_route && !shared.personas {crate::release_requests::Scope::admit(&shared.release_requests,||*lock(&shared.generation));}
   444	    let program = lock(&shared.bound).programs.iter().find(|p| p.program.name == name).map(|p| p.program.clone());
   445	    let program = match program {
   446	        Some(program) if program.kind == ProgramKind::Job => program,
   447	        Some(other) => return refuse(request, 404, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("{name} is a {}, not a job; queries are POST /q/<op>, mutations POST /m/<op>", other.kind.word()))),
   448	        None => return respond(request, 404, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&super::dev_doors::stale(shared, Refusal::new("E_OP", "op", format!("unknown job {name}")).guide("jobs")))})),
   449	    };
   450	    if let Some(raw)=payload.get("args") && let Some(why)=super::dev_doors::persona_argument(shared,&program.args,raw) {return refuse(request,400,why);}
   451	    let mut args = std::collections::BTreeMap::new();
   452	    if let Some(fields) = payload.get("args").and_then(serde_json::Value::as_object) {
   453	        for (field, value) in fields {
   454	            let Some(kind) = program.args.get(field) else { return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("{name} takes no argument {field}"))); };
   455	            match wire::from_json(kind, value) {
   456	                Ok(typed) => { args.insert(field.clone(), typed); }
   457	                Err(why) => return refuse(request, 400, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", format!("argument {field}: {why}"))),
   458	            }
   459	        }
   460	    }
   461	    let mut admitted=lock(&shared.engine);
   462	    crate::release_requests::stamp(admitted.generation());
   463	    if let Some(service)=&service {
   464	        if let Err(why)=super::session_identity::mutation_authority(&request,shared,&admitted,service) {drop(admitted);return refuse(request,403,why);}
   465	    }
   466	    let queued=admitted.enqueue_job(name,args,shared.now());
   467	    drop(admitted);
   468	    crate::release_requests::executed();
   469	    let id = match queued {
   470	        Ok(id) => id,
   471	        Err(error) => return respond(request, 400, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
   472	    };
   473	    // FIFO: earlier queued runs finish first, as with `run` on a stopped store;
   474	    // the background tick may run some chunks too, through the same engine.
   475	    let run = loop {
   476	        let current = {let engine=lock(&shared.engine);crate::release_requests::stamp(engine.generation());engine.job(&id)};
   477	        match current {
   478	            Ok(Some(run)) if matches!(run.state, JobState::Done | JobState::Parked) || crate::lifecycle::stopping() => break run,
   479	            Ok(Some(_)) => {}
   480	            Ok(None) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&RuntimeError::Store(format!("job {id} vanished")).refusal())})),
   481	            Err(error) => return respond(request, 500, json!({"denied":refusal_json(&error.refusal())})),
   482	        }
   483	        let (chunk, logs) = engine_work(shared, |engine, budget| (engine.run_job_chunk_for(shared.now(), budget), engine.take_native_logs()));
   484	        for line in logs { snapback4_core::diagnostic!("snapback4: native {line}"); }
   485	        match chunk {
    35	}
    36	// Rows are written as the system principal; each names its id because seed is an upsert by id.
    37	pub(super) fn seed(request: Request, shared: &Shared, body: &str) {
    38	    if !shared.dev_doors() || !request.remote_addr().is_some_and(|a| a.ip().is_loopback()) {
    39	        respond_timed(request, shared, 403, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&Refusal::new("E_AUTH", "auth", "seeding is a development door")) }));
    40	        return;
    41	    }
    42	    let refuse = |request, refusal: Refusal| respond_timed(request, shared, 400, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&refusal.guide("personas")) }));
    43	    let rows: Vec<Json> = match serde_json::from_str(body) {
    44	        Ok(Json::Array(rows)) => rows,
    45	        _ => return refuse(request, Refusal::new("E_INPUT", "input", "seed takes a JSON array of {table, row}")),
    46	    };
    47	    let mut engine = lock(&shared.engine);
    48	    let now = shared.now();
    49	    let typed = match typed_rows(engine.schema(), &rows, now) {
    50	        Ok(typed) => typed,
    51	        Err(refusal) => { drop(engine); return refuse(request, refusal); }
    52	    };
    53	    let outcome = engine.seed_at(typed, now);
    54	    drop(engine);
    55	    match outcome {
    56	        Ok(count) => {
    57	            shared.durable_committed(&lock(&shared.engine));
    58	            respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "seeded": count }));
    59	        }
    60	        Err(error) => respond_timed(request, shared, 200, json!({ "denied": refusal_json(&seed_refusal(error)) })),
    61	    }

codex
I found a gap in schema-adoption closure: the global door check runs before reading the request body, while clock and system-job handlers still check only whether the host is dev. A request held across adoption could therefore act after the doors close. I also found existing password fixtures that use the now-closed seed door without opting in.

    16	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#2-real-staging-accounts — real credentials and ordinary operator mutations, never system seeding or auth bypass.
    17	export async function seedAccounts(host: Pick<ServeHost, "url" | "operatorToken">, options: SeedAccountsOptions): Promise<StagingSessions> {
    18	  const roles = Object.entries(options.roles), timeout = options.timeoutMs ?? 300_000;
    19	  if (!Number.isSafeInteger(timeout) || timeout < 1 || !roles.length || roles.some(([role, n]) => !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$/.test(role) || !Number.isSafeInteger(n) || n < 1)
    20	      || roles.reduce((sum, [, n]) => sum + n, 0) > 100 || !/^[A-Za-z][A-Za-z0-9_]*$/.test(options.provision?.name ?? "")) {
    21	    throw Error("E_STAGING_INPUT: provide 1..100 accounts across named roles, a mutation handle and a positive timeoutMs");
    22	  }
    23	  const end = performance.now() + timeout;
    24	  const remaining = () => {
    25	    const ms = Math.ceil(end - performance.now());
    26	    if (ms <= 0) throw Error("E_STAGING_TIMEOUT: setup incomplete; partial accounts/grants may exist; inspect or discard this staging deployment");
    27	    return ms;
    28	  };
    29	  const request = (path: string, init?: RequestInit) => fetch(host.url + path, { ...init, signal: AbortSignal.timeout(Math.min(remaining(), 30_000)) });
    30	  const config = await request("/auth/config");
    31	  const identity = await config.json();
    32	  if (!config.ok || identity.environment !== "staging" || identity.development !== false || !identity.password) {
    33	    throw Error("E_STAGING: account seeding requires serve on a fresh deploy --staging with password identity; production and dev refuse before creating users");
    34	  }
    35	  const operator = await host.operatorToken();
    36	  const sessions: StagingSessions = Object.create(null);
    37	  for (const [role, count] of roles) {
    38	    sessions[role] = [];
    39	    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
    40	      const email = `staging-${role}-${randomUUID()}@example.test`, password = randomBytes(24).toString("base64url");
    41	      let reply;
    42	      // Only an explicit HTTP 429 refusal authorizes retrying signup. Network
    43	      // failures and malformed/ambiguous successful replies are never retried.
    44	      for (;;) {
    45	        const response = await request("/auth/signup", { method: "POST", headers: { "content-type": "application/json" }, body: JSON.stringify({ email, password }) });
    46	        reply = await response.json();
    47	        if (response.status !== 429 || reply.denied?.code !== "E_RATE_LIMIT") break;
    48	        await new Promise(resolve => setTimeout(resolve, Math.min(1000, remaining())));
    49	      }
    50	      const session = reply.session;
    51	      if (typeof session?.principal !== "string" || typeof session.token !== "string" || typeof session.expiresAt !== "number") {
    52	        throw Error(`E_STAGING_SIGNUP: signup refused (${reply.denied?.code ?? "invalid reply"}); partial accounts/grants may exist`);
    53	      }

    59	      sessions[role]!.push(account);
    60	    }
    61	  }
    62	  return sessions;

     5	pub(super) const KEY: &str = "deployment_environment";
     6	fn production() -> String {
     7	    "E_STAGING: existing production/legacy stores cannot be relabelled; deploy --staging into a separate fresh directory".into()

    10	// @ref LLP 3000.000.000.001#1-commission-and-boundary — classify only a fresh store under its existing exclusive owner.
    11	pub(super) fn classify(dir: &Path, owner: Option<&Owner>, existed: bool, checking: bool) -> Result<(), String> {
    12	    let path = dir.join("store.sqlite");
    13	    if checking && !existed { return Ok(()); }
    14	    if let Some(owner) = owner {
    15	        let store = owner.open_store(std::time::Duration::from_secs(5)).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    16	        match store.meta(KEY).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() {
    17	            Some("staging") => Ok(()),
    18	            None if !existed && !checking => store.set_meta(KEY, "staging").map_err(|e|e.to_string()),
    19	            _ => Err(production()),
    20	        }
    21	    } else {
    22	        let store = snapback4_core::Store::open_read_only(&path).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?;
    23	        if store.meta(KEY).map_err(|e|e.to_string())?.as_deref() == Some("staging") { Ok(()) } else { Err(production()) }

use super::*;
use std::{fs, path::Path};

const SOURCE: &str = "use personas alice\ntable rows:\n  owner: principal\n  read <- exists grants[parent = .id]\n  online only\ntable grants:\n  parent: rows\n  until: time\n  expire at .until\n  unique byParent: parent\n  read <- deny\n  online only\nquery point(id: rows):\n  return rows[id]\n";
fn exhaust_read_authority(host: &Host) {
    let mut engine = lock(&host.shared.engine);
    let bound = lock(&host.shared.bound);
    let until = host.shared.now() + 3_600_000;
    let mut rows = vec![("rows".into(), snapback4_core::wire::row_from_json(
        &bound.schema.tables["rows"], &json!({"id":"hidden","owner":"bob"})).unwrap(),
    )];
    rows.extend((0..8193).map(|n| {
        ("grants".into(), snapback4_core::wire::row_from_json(
        &bound.schema.tables["grants"], &json!({"id":format!("g{n}"),"parent":"hidden","until":until+n})).unwrap(),
        )
    }));
    engine.load(rows).unwrap();
}
fn latest(project: &Path, op: &str) -> Json {
    let end = Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(10);
    loop {
        if let Ok(records) = dev_refusals::recent(&project.join(".snapback4"), None, None)
            && let Some(record) = records["records"].as_array().unwrap().last()
            && record["operation"] == op { return record.clone(); }
        assert!(Instant::now() < end, "no evidence for {op}");
        thread::sleep(Duration::from_millis(10));
    }
}
#[test]
fn memory_query_branch_collects_and_a_bare_response_cannot_reuse_a_previous_cause() {
    let dir = ReaderTestDirectory::new();
    let host = Host::with_evidence(true, snapback4_core::Store::memory().unwrap(), SOURCE, "127.0.0.1", Some(&dir.0));
    exhaust_read_authority(&host);
    let reply = body(&host.request("POST", "/q/point", "x-snapback-persona: alice\r\n", r#"{"args":{"id":"hidden"}}"#));
    assert_eq!(reply["denied"]["code"], "E_RULE");
    let first = latest(&dir.0, "point");
    assert_eq!(first["clause"], "authorization allowance (read)");
    assert_eq!(first["site"], "schema.q:4:3");
    // Deliberately bypass every operation and collector. Only the shared JSON
    // response layer sees this injected E_RULE, just like a new transport door.
    crate::host_limits::prepare_tests();
    let server = Server::http("127.0.0.1:0").unwrap();
    let address = server.server_addr().to_ip().unwrap();
    let caller = thread::spawn(move || {
        let mut socket = TcpStream::connect(address).unwrap();
        write!(socket, "POST /uninstrumented HTTP/1.1\r\nHost: localhost\r\nConnection: close\r\nContent-Length: 0\r\n\r\n").unwrap();
        let mut reply = String::new(); socket.read_to_string(&mut reply).unwrap(); reply
    });
    let request = server.recv().unwrap();
    let _scope = dev_refusals::response::begin(&host.shared, "/uninstrumented");
    respond(request, 200, json!({"denied":{"code":"E_RULE","message":"injected"}}));
    assert_eq!(body(&caller.join().unwrap())["denied"]["code"], "E_RULE");
    let second = latest(&dir.0, "/uninstrumented");
    assert_eq!(second["clause"], "cause not instrumented");
    assert_eq!(second["code"], "E_RULE");
    assert_ne!(second["requestId"], first["requestId"]);
    assert!(second["site"].is_null());
}

#[test]
fn a_checked_out_reader_keeps_its_source_coordinates_across_publication() {
    let dir = ReaderTestDirectory::new();
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&dir.0.join("reader.db")).unwrap();
    let host = Host::with_evidence(true, store, SOURCE, "127.0.0.1", Some(&dir.0));
    let (_epoch, reader) = host.shared.reader().unwrap();
    let old_generation = reader.generation();
    let next_source = format!("\n\n{SOURCE}\nquery extra():\n  return 1\n");
    let next = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), next_source)]);
    assert!(next.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", next.diagnostics);
    {
        let mut engine = lock(&host.shared.engine);
        engine.adopt(next.schema.clone(), next.programs.iter().map(|p|p.program.clone())).unwrap();
        lock(&host.shared.readers).replace(&engine, &next.refusal_sites);
        *lock(&host.shared.bound) = next;
    }
    assert_eq!(reader.refusal_sites[&("rows".into(), "read rule".into())], "schema.q:4:3");
    let (_, current) = host.shared.reader().unwrap();
    assert_ne!(current.generation(), old_generation);
    assert_eq!(current.refusal_sites[&("rows".into(), "read rule".into())], "schema.q:6:3");
    // Source selection does not consult the now newer shared.bound.
    assert_eq!(fs::read_dir(dir.0.join(".snapback4/dev-refusals")).unwrap().count(), 1);
}

#[test]
fn detached_query_uses_its_checkout_map_when_the_advertised_source_changes() {
    let dir = ReaderTestDirectory::new();
    let store = snapback4_core::Store::open(&dir.0.join("reader.db")).unwrap();
    let host = Host::with_evidence(true, store, SOURCE, "127.0.0.1", Some(&dir.0));
    exhaust_read_authority(&host);
    let generation = lock(&host.shared.engine).generation();
    let shared = Arc::downgrade(&host.shared);
    *lock(&host.shared.query_wait) = Some(Arc::new(move || {
        let shared = shared.upgrade().unwrap();
        if lock(&shared.query_wait).take().is_none() { return; }
        // Pause after checkout and simulate the source-map publication of a save.
        // The reader's schema/transaction remains the generation it checked out.
        *lock(&shared.bound) = snapback4_lang::compile(&[("schema.q".into(), format!("\n\n{SOURCE}"))]);
        *lock(&shared.generation) = generation + 1;
    }));
    let reply = body(&host.request("POST", "/q/point", "x-snapback-persona: alice\r\n", r#"{"args":{"id":"hidden"}}"#));
    assert_eq!(reply["denied"]["code"], "E_RULE");
    let record = latest(&dir.0, "point");
    assert_eq!(record["site"], "schema.q:4:3");
    assert_eq!(record["generation"], generation);
    assert_eq!(lock(&host.shared.bound).refusal_sites[&("rows".into(), "read rule".into())], "schema.q:6:3");
}

        Self::with_capacity_recent(dev, store, source, bind, evidence, crate::host_limits::Capacity::default(), recent)
    }

import { signalChild, spawnTracked as spawn } from '../../../../scripts/test-signals.mjs';
import { captureStdout } from './server-output.mjs';
import { waitFor as awaitState } from './wait.mjs';
import assert from "node:assert/strict";
import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, writeFileSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
import { join, resolve } from "node:path";

export const binary = process.env.SNAPBACK4_BIN ?? resolve(import.meta.dirname, "../../../../target/debug/snapback4");
export const until = async (read, label = "condition", options) => { return awaitState(async () => {
    try { return await read(); }
    catch (error) {
      // Only a cancelled pooled socket is retryable; assertions still fail.
      if (error.cause?.code !== 'UND_ERR_SOCKET') throw error;
    }
  }, label, options); };
export async function server(t, residency = "sync to .owner", source, { watch = false } = {}) {
  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "sb4-app-"));
  mkdirSync(join(dir, "snapback"));
  writeFileSync(join(dir, "snapback/schema.q"), source ?? `use identity
 tablePlaceholder` .replace(' tablePlaceholder', `table notes:
  owner: principal
  body: text <=200
  by byOwner: owner, id
  read <- .owner = viewer
  insert <- .owner = viewer
  ${residency}
mutation add(body: text <=200):
  insert notes { owner: viewer, body }
query list():
  return notes[viewer] first 100 by byOwner
`));
  let output = "";
  const host = spawn(binary, ["dev", "--port", "0", ...(watch ? [] : ["--no-watch"])], { cwd: dir, stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"] });
  const readStdout = captureStdout(host);
  host.stdout.on("data", bytes => { output += bytes; }); host.stderr.on("data", bytes => { output += bytes; });
  t.after(async () => { const done = new Promise(resolve => host.once("exit", resolve)); if (host.exitCode === null && host.signalCode === null) { await signalChild(host, "SIGTERM"); await done; } rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); });
  const url = await until(() => { assert.equal(host.exitCode, null, output); return readStdout().match(/http:\/\/127\.0\.0\.1:\d+(?=\r?\n)/)?.[0]; }, "server");
  return { url, dir };
}

  const path = new URL(url).pathname;
  if (path === '/changes') return parkedChanges(init.signal);
  if (path === '/q/list') queries++;
  if (path === '/auth/refresh') {
   const response = await fetch(url, init); rotated.resolve();
   if (overlapping) await release.promise;
   return response;
  }

   if (!overlapping) await installed.promise;
   const response = await fetch(url, init);

   assert.equal(response.status, 401); assert.equal(obsolete.code, 'E_AUTH');
   // Let discovery's obsolete-refusal handler encounter an in-flight rotation.
   if (overlapping) setTimeout(() => release.resolve(), 20);
   return response;
  }
  return fetch(url, init);

});

   114	    assert.equal((await fetch(host.url + "/q/myRole", { method: "POST", headers: { "x-snapback-persona": "alice" }, body: '{"args":{}}' })).status, 401);
   115	  } finally { await host.stop(); }
   116	});

**NOT READY** — review of `e25de6ae10a1332a3156795bc50e723de1b67686` against `sb4-loop`.

1. **[P1] Dev authority can survive password-only adoption.**  
   [serve.rs:872](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:872) checks door availability before reading the request body. The clock handler subsequently checks only `shared.personas` at [serve_maintenance.rs:353](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve_maintenance.rs:353); system jobs do likewise at line 435. An unauthenticated loopback caller can begin a POST while doors are open, delay completion of its body until password-only adoption closes them, then advance time or enqueue a system job. Seed/reset also check availability before acquiring the engine, leaving an adoption race.

   **Repair:** recheck door authority against the current engine schema while holding the writer guard, immediately before privileged effects. Add deterministic tests covering delayed bodies and requests queued behind adoption. The current adoption test sends requests only after closure.

2. **[P1] The CLI unit-test target does not compile at this commit.**  
   [serve.rs:103](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/src/serve.rs:103) adds required field `unsafe_dev_doors`, but the `Shared` initializer at [serve_refusals/mod.rs:139](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/serve_refusals/mod.rs:139) omits it. This module is included under `cfg(test)`, so compiling that target produces Rust E0063. Updating the separate poison-test initializer does not cover it.

   **Repair:** initialize the field explicitly in this fixture, choosing its door policy deliberately, then compile and run affected CLI tests. This is an implementation defect, not outstanding platform qualification.

3. **[P2] `seedAccounts` rejects ordinary generated modular mutation handles.**  
   The validation at [proof-accounts.ts:20](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/packages/snapback4/src/proof-accounts.ts:20) accepts only unqualified identifiers. Generated handles for mutations outside `schema.q` carry names such as `accounts.provision`; the emitter preserves that qualified name at [emit.rs:168](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit.rs:168). Passing such a handle immediately throws `E_STAGING_INPUT`, contrary to the advertised generated-handle contract.

   **Repair:** accept the compiler’s qualified operation-name syntax and test provisioning from a separate Quarry module. I confirmed the rejection using the actual helper source with mocked transport and no filesystem writes.

4. **[P2] Existing affected fixtures and assertions remain incompatible with the new defaults.**  
   [witness/lib.mjs:60](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/witness/lib.mjs:60) launches dev without an unsafe-door opt-in, then unconditionally seeds at line 84 without checking refusal. [web-session-authority.test.mjs:55](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/packages/snapback4/test/web-session-authority.test.mjs:55) uses this helper with a password-only schema, so its audit seed is now refused; its later private-note seed is refused too. Additionally, [lifecycle.rs:772](/home/ccheever/projects/sb4-b16-stage/snapback4/crates/snapback4/tests/lifecycle.rs:772) expects the previous seed-refusal message, and line 779 expects the removed banner.

   **Repair:** provide an explicit fixture-only unsafe-door option, use it in affected password fixtures, assert seed success, and update the changed diagnostic assertions.

The inspected production path retains bearer authentication and the protected local token protocol. The first-admin recipe independently restricts roster insertion to `service:operator`. In-memory helper checks passed for production refusal before token minting, the normal provisioning sequence, and avoiding ambiguous-signup retries.

Separately, packed-guide execution, Darwin qualification, full crate results, and the three converted wave-16 probes remain unverified here. These are qualification obligations, distinct from the defects above. This commit’s Linux qualifier does not invoke the staging recipe.

No files were edited. No Cargo commands or full `scripts/test.sh` were run. Reviewer outputs and `WORKSTREAM-REPORT.md` were not read; subsequent working-tree changes are outside this verdict.
